# CatCode migration — STATUS dashboard

**This is the single source of truth for "what's done."** A session asked to report migration
progress reads THIS file first. When a session finishes a unit of work, it updates the row here
(status + date + one-line note) as its last step.

Status legend: ⬜ not started · 🟡 in progress · ✅ done · 🛑 blocked
Model tag: **CLAUDE** = visual-design / system-architecture judgment · **ANY** = model-agnostic.
Difficulty: 1–10 (operator picks reasoning effort from it).
**🖐 GUI** = stamped only on sessions whose verification needs the operator to drive a live
GUI/browser step (launch the Electron app, click, keypress); absent = fully headless-verifiable.

> **When a phase's sessions are all ✅, the next phase's backlog gets generated** (per
> PROGRAM-PLAN §8) — with the 🧠 Model/Difficulty tag stamped on every task it produces.
> Phases 4–5 are NOT split into sessions yet; they appear here only as coarse rows until opened.

---

| **CC-20 · Accounts page could not show account usage (global read was on the per-session transport)** → `docs/migration/decisions/ACCOUNTS-OWNERSHIP.md`; `app/shared/accountsPoolWorker.ts` (new), `app/sidecar/accountsPoolWorker.ts` (new), `app/main/accountsPoolRunner.ts` (new) | ✅ **LANDED 2026-07-28** (local `migration`, not pushed) · ⬜ **operator GUI acceptance PENDING** | **Operator report:** "What even is this Account page? why it doesn't look like the prototype at all" — the page rendered only its waiting state. **First diagnosis was too shallow and was withdrawn:** `App.tsx:2483` passed `selectAccountsSnapshot(accounts, activeSessionId)` while Accounts is an unconditional sidebar item (`Sidebar.tsx:140`), so with no active session the selector returned null; the one-line fix would have been the `?? selectFirstAccountsSnapshot` fallback the two other consumers already use (`App.tsx:1862,2556`). **That fix is wrong** — it hides the defect by borrowing an arbitrary session's stale copy. **Real mechanism:** the pool has NO session dependency (`accountsDomain.ts:56-70` reads `getPoolStatus()`/`getClaudePoolStatus()`, bare module singletons over the `~/.cat-code/` vault; neither takes a session), but the sidecar is one process per session (N-process, LOCKED) and `HostApi` carried only session lifecycle with no read seam, so P4-5 shipped a global read as a per-session domain — the recipe its own header calls "the CANONICAL W4 domain read-seam recipe". Three consequences: (1) no session ⇒ no data; (2) N sessions ⇒ N in-memory copies ⇒ different numbers for the same account; (3) **fatal for a usage page** — `refreshAccountsUsageOnce()` is guarded one-shot per sidecar (`sidecarServer.ts:2569`) and after that re-broadcasts only on that session's own pool-mutating verb, so the page showed the number its session loaded at spawn, forever. **Landed:** accounts is now a host-plane read, structurally identical to `CATALOG-OWNERSHIP` shape (b) and reusing its machinery — disposable engine-graph worker → bounded NDJSON → main-side fail-closed parse + `secretGuard` re-scan → read-only `accounts-pool` host event → global slot in `accountsState`. Cadence **60 s** (vs the catalog's 30 s) because headroom is coarse and the worker's one network read is engine-side 1-min-cached. Redaction is REUSED, not re-derived: the worker calls the already-exported pure `buildAccountsSnapshot` (`accountsDomain.ts:430`), per CLAUDE.md §8 rule 10. **The deliberate deviation from both sibling workers (CC-21 later gave them this same bootstrap, for the same reason):** they call `initializeSidecarRuntime()`; this one must not, because full `init()` fires `void initAccountPool()` (`src/entrypoints/init.ts:90`) which starts periodic token refresh, quarantine probes and a startup `touchAll()` — on a 60 s timer that is real credential rotation forever. It instead uses the engine's OWN observation-only entry point `loadPoolForObservation()` (`codexAccountPool.ts:171-181`, extracted upstream for exactly this caller class: "without triggering token refresh, quarantine probes, or usage polls") plus `enableConfigs()`; `initializeRuntime.ts` was split to export the MACRO shim alone rather than duplicate it. **Caught only by running it:** the first build failed live with `[accounts-worker] fatal: Config accessed before allowed` (`config.ts:1465`) — the observation path still reads global config; surfaced by the hardening smoke actually spawning the worker, not by any unit test. **Verified end-to-end against the REAL vault** (read-only): one record, 2,902 B, 5 Codex + 2 Anthropic accounts with live usage, parses through the main-side boundary, `secretGuard` clean, and zero occurrences of `accessToken`/`refreshToken`/`vaultFilePath`/`idToken` in the raw bytes. **Tests:** 34 new (16 boundary accept/reject incl. a token-smuggling row rejected outright, 13 runner accept/fail-closed/driver-cadence, 5 renderer covering the exact regression — pool populates with `state.sessions === {}`, global outranks session, session teardown never clears it). **Battery:** `bun test app/` **1709 pass / 0 fail** (was 1675 pre-change) · `typecheck` clean · `typecheck:sidecar` 0 owned (5546 upstream ignored) · `test:hardening` **19/19** · `renderer:build` ok · `maps:lint` passed · `git diff --check` clean. **Security:** outbound-only on the EXISTING `catcode:host:event` channel — no new preload channel, no new inbound vocabulary, no protocol version bump; writes stay session-plane `account.*` verbs with their T6 re-resolve intact. **Also fixed:** the waiting-state copy printed engineering notes at the user ("does not use prototype fixtures… once the sidecar sends the real redacted Codex account pool"), the §7 / `DeferredNote` violation; | **CC-18 · Sidebar workspace groups are drag-reorderable, and the order sticks** → `app/renderer/src/sidebarWorkspaceOrder.ts` (new), `app/renderer/src/Sidebar.tsx` | ✅ **LANDED 2026-07-27** (`78e920b`, local `migration`, not pushed) · headless-only · ⬜ operator GUI acceptance PENDING | **Operator request:** *"the workspace on left side bar should be draggable to reorder the order of workspace"*. ⚠ **Id note:** commit `78e920b` says `CC-17` — a concurrent session claimed that id in STATUS seconds later; the commit message was NOT rewritten (§4 no-history-rewrite on a shared branch). This row is the authority. **➕ real-added, NOT parity** — the prototype sidebar has no workspace reordering at all (its only drag is a SESSION into a split pane, `Sidebar.jsx:246`), so there is no prototype anchor; it adds no element the `PARITY-LEDGER.md` tracks, so the ledger was correctly left untouched. **Design — one pure module, thin DOM over it.** `sidebarWorkspaceOrder.ts` owns the whole decision: `selectOrderedWorkspaceGroups` (ranked-first, then the incoming frozen-alphabetical remainder, Unknown bucket pinned last), `selectWorkspaceDropEdge`, `reduceWorkspaceOrderMoved`, `reduceWorkspaceOrderStepped`, plus the versioned read/write pair. Forced by the `app/renderer` suite being SSR-only (`renderToStaticMarkup` — it cannot fire a drag, a pointer event, or an effect); `composerState.ts` (CC-16) is the precedent for the split. **Constraints honoured, verified in source:** (1) the shared `groupByWorkspace` (`sessionsCatalogState.ts:508`, used by BOTH `Sidebar.tsx:203` and `SessionsPage.tsx:37`) is byte-identical — the custom order is applied to its RESULT on the sidebar side, so the Sessions page keeps frozen-alphabetical; (2) the order is keyed on **`cwd`, never the rendered label**, because `disambiguateWorkspaceLabels` (CC-14/CC-15) rewrites labels the moment a basename collides — pinned by a test that opens a colliding workspace and asserts the placement survives `app` → `PTClove/app`; (3) **`activeCwd` is never read** — the CC-2 warp-free rule is intact and asserted directly; (4) **renderer-local persistence only** — versioned JSON at `catcode.sidebarWorkspaceOrder.v1` through an injectable `Pick<Storage,…>` (the `reasoningLayout.ts` idiom), best-effort, capped at 64 entries. **No protocol frame, no preload channel, no host/registry field, no inbound vocabulary** (SECURITY-MINIMUM §2). **Decisions owned:** *Unknown workspace* (`cwd === ''`) is **pinned last** and is neither drag source nor drop target — it is not a workspace but the MAJOR-1 catch-all, its membership churns, and `''` is a fragile persisted key; an order entry for an **absent** workspace is **kept but skipped** (the normal case as the search box filters groups in and out, and how a rank survives a relaunch that has not enumerated the workspace yet), so the rail degrades to alphabetical rather than throwing or reshuffling; a **new** workspace joins **after** everything the operator placed, displacing nothing; a **keyboard path exists** (⌥↑/⌥↓ on the focused header, advertised via `aria-keyshortcuts`) because drag-only is inaccessible and Alt+Arrow was unclaimed in the renderer. **Drag payload is `text/workspace-cwd`**, deliberately distinct from the tab→split `text/sessionId` (`TabBar.tsx:265`), so the two drags can never cross-trigger. **Drop indicator** is a static-class 2px accent rule (`h-[2px] bg-accent`, the TabBar-underline idiom) on the exact edge the reducer will use, plus `opacity-50` on the source — **all static classes, verified PRESENT in the built CSS** (`app/renderer/dist/assets/index-*.css`), so the Tailwind-v4 interpolated-arbitrary-value trap is avoided by construction. **Red-before-green by bounded mutation, restored byte-for-byte (sha `6e184c21…` / `c297d28e…`):** ignoring the order in `selectOrderedWorkspaceGroups` → 7 fail; making the Unknown bucket rankable → 5 fail; ignoring drag direction in `selectWorkspaceDropEdge` → 5 fail; dropping `draggable` from the header → 1 fail. **Coverage:** empty order · ranked+unranked mix · newcomer joins · absent entries · Unknown pinned both ways · both drag directions · adjacent swap · no-op identity (same reference returned) · a search-hidden workspace keeping its rank · reducer↔indicator agreement · keyboard step both ways, both ends, and past the pinned bucket · storage round-trip, corrupt/wrong-version/junk/throwing store · the cap · and a full drop→persist→relaunch→render pass over the REAL `groupByWorkspace`. **Battery (2026-07-27, shared dirty tree):** `bun test app/` **1519 pass / 0 fail** (133 files; baseline 1483 at `2f4278d` — +28 mine, the rest from concurrent sessions) · `bun run --cwd app typecheck` clean · `typecheck:sidecar` green (5,546 upstream ignored, 0 owned) · `test:hardening` **19/19** · `renderer:build` ✓ (589 modules) · `git diff --check` clean. **Proof layer: helper + SSR-render. The DOM, the drag gesture itself, real `localStorage`, and restart persistence are UNVERIFIED** — operator GUI steps are listed in the Phase-5 check list below. |

| **CC-16 · Opening a not-live session made the user wait on, and act for, the engine spawn** → `app/renderer/src/App.tsx`, `app/renderer/src/composerState.ts` | ✅ **LANDED 2026-07-27** (local `migration`, not pushed) · headless-only · ⬜ operator GUI acceptance PENDING | **Operator report:** *"When open unconnected session. There is a small time that it show as 'connecting…' or state before focus the composer 'focus to connect'. I dont think we need it. **User should not feel anything.**"* **Root cause:** one flag, `composerEnabled` (pre-fix `App.tsx:2713-2718`), conflated two unrelated reasons the composer could be unusable — *"no engine attached yet"* (preview pane / `connecting` / `starting`) and *"the engine says input is closed"* (`ready` + `inputEnabled:false`, i.e. mid-turn). Because they were one flag, the transient case inherited the mid-turn treatment: `readOnly={!composerEnabled}` (`App.tsx:3201`) refused every keystroke for the ~0.6 s cold-connect floor, and the placeholder cascade (`App.tsx:3220-3224`) *instructed* the user — `Focus to reconnect…`, then `Connecting…`. **Fix — separate the two, then hide the spawn behind the typing the user is already doing.** New pure model in `composerState.ts`: `selectComposerGate` returns `{engineInputEnabled, connectPending, editable}`; `editable = engineInputEnabled ‖ connectPending` drives all three composer consumers (textarea `readOnly`, send arrow, attach) so "typeable" can never drift from "sendable" again. Typing, the send arrow and attach are live while connect-pending; **only the submit waits** — `planSessionSubmit` returns `hold`, `submitSession` parks the paste-EXPANDED text per session (`reducePendingSubmitHeld`, keyed like `promptDrafts`) and retires the draft exactly like a real send, and a `useEffect` drains it through the **existing** `app.submit` the moment `resolvePendingSubmit` sees ready+input-enabled. **NOT spawn-on-open** — the CC-5 §I.1 ruling-#3 dwell removal stands untouched; the spawn still fires only on intent (`engagePreviewPane` on focus/pointer-down, now also on form-submit so a send-arrow click on a pre-filled draft can't wait on a spawn nobody started). **Failure is observable, never silent:** every terminal outcome — the three failure paths of `restoreLiveSession` and any terminal connection status (`dead`/`failed`/`exited`/`disconnected`) — calls `releasePendingSubmit`, which puts the text BACK in the composer (`restoreDraftWithPending` prepends it without clobbering anything typed since) and raises `PENDING_SUBMIT_RELEASED_MESSAGE`. **Mid-turn is untouched and pinned:** two regression guards (`composerState.test.ts` gate + plan, `App.test.tsx` SSR) assert a `ready`+`inputEnabled:false` composer is still read-only with its send arrow disabled on a NON-empty draft, and both survived the pre-fix mutation — proving they encode unchanged behaviour. **§0 fidelity (proposal, not a decision):** the prototype composer (`~/catcode_prototype/cat-app/Chat.jsx:1395-1424`, placeholder `Chat.jsx:1145`) models **no** disconnected/connecting state at all — it is always editable and its only disabled affordance is `disabled={!input.trim()}` on send. The removed strings were therefore desktop **inventions**, so this change moves TOWARD parity, and the ledger rows L479/L480 are updated. Residual: `Connecting…` still shows on the terminal statuses (dead/failed/exited/disconnected), which is a pre-existing lie this change deliberately did not widen — proposed as a separate copy fix. **Supersedes** the M3 clause of `docs/migration/specs/2026-07-14-instant-session-open-design.md:133` ("composer … stays readOnly … quiet 'connecting…' hint"), the same way CC-5 superseded that spec's 300 ms dwell; the dated spec is historical and was not rewritten. **Evidence:** red-before-green by bounded mutation + byte-identical restore (sha256 verified) — reverting the three gate bindings + the placeholder cascade fails 3 `App.test.tsx` tests; forcing `connectPending = false` fails 6 `composerState.test.ts` tests; dropping ONE of the three `releasePendingSubmit` calls fails the wiring tripwire. **Proof layer: helper + SSR-render; the DOM/focus/keystroke and effect-drain layers are UNVERIFIED** — `app/renderer` has no happy-dom (adding one needs sign-off), so no test here can press a key or run a React effect. **Battery:** `bun test app/` 1483/0 (baseline 1456/0, +27) · `bun run --cwd app typecheck` clean · `typecheck:sidecar` green (5,546 upstream ignored, 0 new owned) · `test:hardening` 19/19 · `renderer:build` ok. Sidecar count 0→0 (the hardening smoke's own sidecar was reaped). **PENDING operator GUI:** open a not-live session from the sidebar, type immediately — the first keystroke must land with no `Focus to reconnect…`/`Connecting…` and no dropped characters; press Enter within the connect window and confirm the turn starts once ready; then confirm a mid-turn composer still refuses input. |
| **CC-15 · Welcome-launcher recents carried the same basename collision the sidebar just fixed** → `app/renderer/src/sessionsCatalogState.ts` | ✅ **LANDED 2026-07-26** (local `migration`, not pushed) · headless-only · ⬜ operator GUI acceptance PENDING | **The follow-up CC-14 deliberately left.** `selectRecentWorkspaces` keys the launcher's recent-projects list by `row.cwd` (correct) but labelled every entry `name: basename(row.cwd) || row.cwd` (pre-fix `sessionsCatalogState.ts:583`) — the identical defect, on the surface where the operator PICKS a project to open rather than one where they pick among rows they already recognise, and where each entry carries a per-path `trusted` badge joined from `workspace-trust.snapshot` (`App.tsx:883-892`), so two same-named entries could offer *different* trust decisions under one name (the CC-9 trust-scope confusion class). Latent only because `limit = 6` caps the list. **Fix — the helper was REUSED, not adapted and not forked:** `disambiguateWorkspaceLabels` (`sessionsCatalogState.ts:449-484`, module-private, same file) already takes `readonly string[]` → `Map<cwd, label>` and needed **zero** change; `groupByWorkspace` is byte-identical and its 5 CC-14 tests are untouched and still pass. Depth 1 of the helper *is* `basename(cwd) \|\| cwd`, so an uncontested recent's label is unchanged by construction. **Decision — disambiguate AFTER the cap, over exactly the ≤6 entries that render.** The helper's contract is "shortest label separating it from the others ON SCREEN", and `groupByWorkspace` hands it precisely the set it returns; matching that keeps ONE invariant for both call sites. A 7th, never-rendered `/other/app` must not widen a visible label to resolve a collision the operator cannot see, and because the capped set is a subset the after-cap labels are always equal-or-shorter, never more ambiguous. **This is pinned executably, not by comment:** re-ordering the two statements to disambiguate before the cap makes `47 pass → 46 pass / 1 fail` (`Expected "app", Received "cat-code/app"`); production restored byte-for-byte (md5 `0342ff81…`). **Preserved verbatim:** cwd keying, newest-first `modifiedAtMs` sort, the `limit` slice, `appSessionId`/`live` openable-adoption, `sessionCount` accumulation, the `trusted` join, and the empty-cwd (MAJOR-1) label — asserted alongside the new label claims, not assumed. **Read-time only — no new field, no protocol/sidecar/preload/inbound-vocabulary change** (every cwd is already in hand). **⚠ Premise correction — where the label is actually read.** The dispatch brief assumed the dropdown ROWS render `name`; they do not. `RecentItem` renders the full `recent.cwd` (`WelcomeScreen.tsx:296`, prototype-parity "folder icon + mono path", ledger `:1988`), so the untrusted badge sits beside a full path today and was never ambiguous there. `RecentWorkspace.name`'s **only** current consumer is the collapsed picker TRIGGER (`WelcomeScreen.tsx:219` `recents[0]!.name`) — which is exactly where "which `app` am I in?" is unanswerable, and which now reads `cat-code/app`. The fix is therefore correct and useful, but its live blast radius is one label, and `name` is now right for any future consumer. **➕ Flagged, NOT fixed (needs an owner):** (a) a recent with an empty cwd yields `name: ''`, so the trigger renders a blank button — pre-existing, orthogonal to collisions, and giving it a "Unknown workspace"-style string is a new user-visible string the prototype does not have, so it was not invented here; (b) whether the dropdown rows should switch from full cwd to the disambiguated `name` is a *parity* question (the prototype pins mono path) and is left to the operator, not silently changed. **Red-before-green (proved by reverting, not asserted):** with the production hunk reverted to `9ee891d` via `git show` while the new tests stayed, all 5 new tests fail (`42 pass / 5 fail`) — the operator's collision returns `Received: "app"` for both entries, the different-trust case returns `Received length: 2` for "entries answering to the untrusted label", and the trailing-slash case returns `"app"`; restored byte-for-byte (md5 verified) → `47 pass / 0 fail`. **Coverage:** operator's real two-way collision · uncontested basename stays bare while a neighbour widens · collision with different `trusted` + openable-id adoption riding along · cap boundary in both directions (twin cut ⇒ survivor stays bare; cap raised ⇒ both widen) · degenerate cwds (trailing slash still collides, `basename('/') === ''` still renders `/`, empty cwd unchanged). All five go through the REAL `selectMergedSessionRows` merge — the production feed at `App.tsx:894` — not hand-built rows. **Battery (re-measured 2026-07-26 on a shared dirty tree; baseline `bun test app/` 1451/0 before the change):** `bun test app/` **1456 pass / 0 fail** (132 files, +5) · `bun run --cwd app typecheck` clean · `typecheck:sidecar` passed (5546 upstream ignored, **0 owned**) · `test:hardening` **19/19** · `renderer:build` ✓ (588 modules) · `git diff --check` clean. One sidecar I spawned (pid 7004) SIGTERM'd per the CC-3 kill-what-you-spawn rule; the other PPID-1 orphan predates this session and was left alone. **Proof layer: helper/selector (pure read-time projection).** DOM and GUI **UNVERIFIED** — the `app/` renderer suite is SSR-only, so the trigger label was not observed rendered. Operator GUI step: launch with two projects sharing a basename in the six most-recent workspaces, open the Welcome launcher, and read the collapsed Project trigger — it must show `cat-code/app`, not `app`. Ledger row updated (`PARITY-LEDGER.md:1986`, ProjectValue trigger button). |
| **CC-14 · Two sidebar workspace groups both labelled `APP`, adjacent and indistinguishable** → `app/renderer/src/sessionsCatalogState.ts`, `app/renderer/src/Sidebar.tsx` | ✅ **LANDED 2026-07-26** (`9ee891d`, local `migration`, not pushed) · ✅ **operator GUI-verified 2026-07-26** — sidebar rendered `CAT-CODE/APP` + `PTCLOVE/APP` in place of two identical `APP` headings, with non-colliding groups (`CAT-CODE`, `DISCORDBOT`, `TMP`) left bare (operator: *"looking good"*) | **Bug (operator-reported):** the rail showed two stacked `APP` groups — `/Users/pt/cat-code/app` (12 transcripts) and `/Users/pt/PTClove/app` (1) — with nothing to tell them apart; the operator nearly deleted the wrong one. **Root cause, re-verified in source:** `groupByWorkspace` groups on `row.cwd` (correct — the full path IS the workspace identity, so those genuinely are two groups) but labelled each `name: cwd ? basename(cwd) || cwd : 'Unknown workspace'` (pre-fix `sessionsCatalogState.ts:444`), discarding everything that distinguished the two paths, and then sorted `a.name.localeCompare(b.name)` (`:448`) so the colliding labels landed adjacent — ambiguous became confusing. **Fix — disambiguate only when ambiguous** (the VS Code/JetBrains idiom): a basename nobody contests stays bare (`discordbot`), a colliding one gets the SHORTEST leading path that separates it (`cat-code/app` vs `PTClove/app`). Widening is **progressive, not a fixed one parent segment** — `/a/x/app` and `/b/x/app` both end `x/app`, so a one-segment rule would reproduce the exact defect; each round widens only the still-colliding labels (`disambiguateWorkspaceLabels`, `sessionsCatalogState.ts:469-506`). Exhausting a path falls back to the whole cwd, which is unique by construction *because the cwd is the group key* — that is what bounds the loop. Degenerate cases pinned by test: trailing slash (`/…/app/` still collides with `/…/app`), `basename('/') === ''` still renders `/` not a blank header, empty cwd stays in the `Unknown workspace` bucket and is held OUT of disambiguation (no path to widen). **Read-time only — no new field, no protocol/sidecar/preload change, no inbound vocabulary** (`groupByWorkspace` already receives every cwd). **Ordering:** now sorts on the DISAMBIGUATED label, so what the operator reads stays alphabetical; it remains a pure function of the cwd set, so the FROZEN-order ruling (operator 2026-07-21 — opening a session must never float its workspace) is intact and is asserted directly (order identical for `activeCwd` null / each of 3 cwds). `.localeCompare` gains a `cwd` tiebreak for a total order. **Both consumers fixed by construction** — `Sidebar.tsx:426` and `SessionsPage.tsx:236` both render `group.name`, so zero component change was needed; `rg` found no third consumer. **Prototype gives no answer and none was invented:** `~/catcode_prototype/cat-app/Sidebar.jsx:26` groups on `s.workspace`, a mock STRING label with no path in its data model, so a basename collision is structurally impossible in its fixtures — the documented "prototype mock data is never the contract" case (CLAUDE.md §6). Recorded in the code doc-comment and in 4 `PARITY-LEDGER.md` rows (`:224`, `:225`, `:1187`, `:1189`) so it is not re-litigated. **Red-before-green (proved by reverting, not asserted):** with the production hunk reverted byte-for-byte, all 5 new tests fail (`37 pass / 5 fail`) — the collision case returns `Received: "app"` for both groups and the frozen-order case returns `["app","app","zeta"]`; restored (sha-verified `075f794c…`) → `42 pass / 0 fail`. **Battery (re-measured 2026-07-26 on a shared dirty tree):** `bun test app/` **1451 pass / 0 fail** (132 files) · `bun run --cwd app typecheck` clean · `typecheck:sidecar` passed (5546 upstream ignored, 0 owned) · `test:hardening` **19/19** · `renderer:build` ✓ · `git diff --check` clean. **⬜ Residual (flagged, not fixed — out of scope):** `selectRecentWorkspaces` (`sessionsCatalogState.ts:562`) labels the Welcome launcher's recents with the same bare `basename(row.cwd) || row.cwd` and carries the identical latent ambiguity; it is a different selector on a different surface — proposed owner P4-17 follow-on. Two stale `Sidebar.tsx` comments still claiming "current-workspace-first" corrected in passing. |
| **CC-13 · Settings → Permissions was stuck on "Waiting for the engine's permission context…" forever, and its "Default mode" heading showed a session's live mode** → `app/renderer/src/PermissionRulesEditor.tsx`, `app/renderer/src/SettingsShell.tsx`, `app/renderer/src/settingsState.ts`, `app/shared/protocol.ts`, `app/sidecar/settingsDomain.ts` | ✅ **LANDED 2026-07-26** (local `migration`, not pushed) · ⬜ operator GUI acceptance PENDING | **Bug (operator-reported, re-verified in source):** with no session attached, the whole Permissions pane was one never-resolving line. `PermissionRulesEditor.tsx:35-41` early-returned "Waiting for the engine's permission context…" whenever `context === null`; `SettingsShell.tsx:349-364` fed it ONLY `permissionContext`, which `App.tsx:2304` derives as `selectPermissionContext(permissions, activeSessionId)` — and `activeSessionId` initialises to `null` (`App.tsx:365`), so the selector returns null (`permissionState.ts:303-309`) and nothing ever arrives. **Second, independent defect:** the section headed "Default mode" rendered `context.mode` — the session's live RESOLVED mode, not any default. It looks right on a cold launch (the session usually opens at the default) and silently diverges the moment the mode is changed from the composer. **The real setting was unreachable from the desktop app entirely:** `permissions.defaultMode` (`src/utils/settings/types.ts:59`) had no surface. **⚠ One premise in the dispatch brief was WRONG and is corrected here:** the brief named `snapshot: SettingsSnapshot | null` (`SettingsShell.tsx:165`) as "the correct seam… four lines above the wrong one", implying it is session-independent. It is **not** — `App.tsx:2315` keys it on the same `activeSessionId`, `settingsState.ts:63-69` returns null for a null id, and the frame is emitted per-sidecar at attach *after* the C3 context (`sidecarServer.ts:509-517`). The settings seam is therefore strictly **less** available than the permission seam, never more; switching source alone could not have fixed the reported symptom. What actually fixes it is removing the all-or-nothing gate, which is orthogonal to which seam feeds the value. **Judgement call on the rules half — SPLIT, and the split lands almost entirely on the mode side.** The rules pane's session-scoped sourcing is **correct and stays**: the C3 context is a strict SUPERSET of the settings files that no settings-file read can reproduce — runtime `cliArg`/`command` rules, C1 always-allow suggestions applied mid-turn, PermissionRequest hooks applying rules, auto-mode dangerous-rule stripping (`sidecar/sessionController.ts` post-steps), and above all `managedRulesOnly` (`permissionDomain.ts:35` → `shouldAllowManagedPermissionRulesOnly()`), which can make user/project/local/CLI rules **not load at all** — so rendering settings-file rules there would actively misreport what is enforced. `ruleMetadata` (P4-34) is likewise derived at the sidecar with the engine's own rule parser (`protocol.ts:562-572`) precisely so the renderer never learns the grammar, and `permissionClassifierEnabled` is the runtime gate alone, not a setting (it was a build-time feature gate ∧ runtime gate until CC-21 found the macro always reads false in the unbundled sidecar). Only `permissions.defaultMode` is a persisted global, and only it moved. **Landed:** additive read-only `SettingsSnapshot.permissionDefaultMode = { value, source }` on the EXISTING `settings.snapshot` outbound frame (the CC-9 `trustRoot` precedent) — no new frame kind, no preload channel, no write verb, no version bump. Deliberately NOT added to `EDITABLE_SETTINGS`: that list gates the sidecar write path, and a permission mode must stay read-only (T6b; `permission.setMode` has no `defaultMode` destination, PERMISSION-BOUNDARY.md §3, `permissionDomain.ts:44-48`), with a test asserting the write is refused. **Nested-key precedence handled explicitly:** the engine deep-merges settings (`mergeWith(..., settingsMergeCustomizer)`, `settings.ts:848`), so `defaultMode` resolves on its OWN axis, not on the builder's top-level `seen` set — a higher layer setting only `permissions.allow` must not shadow a lower layer's `defaultMode`, and a test pins exactly that. **Pane restructured:** "Default mode" now shows the SETTING + a `SourceBadge`, or an explicit "not set" (never a borrowed session value); the live mode moved under a "This session" heading keeping its `Current permission mode: <mode>` aria-label; the session-derived sections render only with a context, and their absence is a **terminal statement** ("No session is attached…"), never a wait. **P4-34 Lane 2 is now reachable** — match-type labels, managed-policy state and classifier state were behind this gate and have never been seen live; they still require a session, but the pane no longer blocks the route to them. **Failing-then-passing:** 9 new tests, all 9 fail against HEAD production code restored via `git show` (e.g. *Expected to contain "Default permission mode: plan"*), all 9 pass after; the 11 pre-existing tests in those two files pass in BOTH states; production files restored byte-for-byte (md5 verified). Live-path proof included: a real `settings.json` read through the engine's own `getSettingsForSource` inside `createSidecarSettingsDomain`, not a fixture. **Battery:** `bun test app/` **1446 pass / 0 fail** (baseline re-measured 1433 before the change; +13) · `typecheck` clean · `typecheck:sidecar` 0 owned (5546 upstream ignored) · `test:hardening` **19/19** · `renderer:build` ok. **⬜ Residual, stated precisely — the part the constraints forbid closing:** with **zero** sessions ever attached this window still has no engine data at all, so the Default mode section reads "not set" rather than the true persisted value. That is a program-level property of the N-process model (settings reach the renderer only via a sidecar), not of this pane — the General/Managed/Agents panes are equally empty then. Closing it needs a non-session settings read on the host control plane, i.e. a new preload channel, which this task explicitly forbade; **stopped and reported rather than built**. Ledger rows updated (`PARITY-LEDGER.md` §Permissions: the "Waiting…" ux-state row replaced, new default-mode data-binding row added). |

(3) **one-shot cleanup** `app/scripts/reap-orphan-sidecars.ts` (`bun run --cwd app reap:orphans`) — dry-run default, `--confirm` SIGTERMs PPID-1 orphans + removes stale `/tmp/catcode-<pid>` dirs, keeps sidecars with a live parent. Security baseline untouched (no new inbound vocabulary/preload). **No new inbound frames** — supervisor↔sidecar socket count unchanged across a full suite run. **Cleanup DONE (operator-confirmed 2026-07-09):** `reap:orphans --confirm` SIGTERM'd **43/43** orphans + removed **44/44** stale dirs (~2.5 GB freed; 0 remaining). | **CC-4 · Per-session cost evaluation + shared-engine spike** → `docs/migration/decisions/PER-SESSION-COST.md` | 📋 **EVALUATED 2026-07-14** · N-process UNCHANGED | **Why:** operator — "cost per session should not be this much." **Measured (not estimated):** a live session is ~200 MB+ RSS / ~1–3 s spawn, dominated by a **~189 MB engine-code load paid per process** (the N-process tax; Bun 21 MB → 210 MB after importing the session graph — more RAM than the whole app shell: Electron-main ~135 + renderer ~70). **Verdict:** that floor is INHERENT to N-process and NOT reducible by worker threads (each re-imports the graph); the only lever is a shared engine = **reopening P0-4**, blocked by the engine's process-global state (cwd in `src/utils/Shell.ts` `setCwd`:447 + session id in `src/bootstrap/state.ts`, mutated per-submit at `src/QueryEngine.ts:245`) — the exact out-of-scope refactor P0-4 cited (its own `state.ts:435` anchor has since drifted; verified anchors in the doc). **Reframe:** the model is already cheap AT REST (a restorable row = 0 process cost); cost is purely how many sessions are LIVE at once — which is why aggressive auto-reconnect-on-launch (parked) is the wrong trade. **Recommendation:** keep N-process; manage concurrency (live-session cap + CC-3 idle-TTL); cheap wins if launch latency bites (load sessions-catalog once in host vs per-sidecar; lazy-load MCP/codex-core; bytecode bundle). Reopen criteria + cheap-wins list in the doc. Not a decision — the standing answer to "why is a session so heavy." |

This also removes a latent unbounded-period bug: anchoring on completion let the period grow to `SESSIONS_CATALOG_WORKER_TIMEOUT_MS` (a 5.5-minute period for a hung run), silently degrading the CATALOG-OWNERSHIP §4 "one interval + one boot" contract. | **CC-6 · Browsing history no longer evicts restorable sessions: registry bound 32 → 256 + HC4 cap split** → `app/host/registry.ts`, `app/shared/hostApi.ts`, `app/host/host.ts`, `app/main/devHarness.ts` | ✅ **LANDED 2026-07-26** (merged `1178d0a` → pushed `ba23218`) | **Closes P4-28 operator findings (2) + (3)** (that row untouched — this is the owning entry). **Bug:** opening a session from history mints a registry row; at the bound `enforceBound` evicts the oldest terminal row, which is a genuinely-restorable recently-closed session — so a **read** action destroyed state, with nothing signalling it (the 2026-07-20 bare-rows ruling removed status chips). Operator's live `registry.json` sat at 32/32 (1 live, 31 closed) on 2026-07-21: permanently at the cap, evicting on every open. **Operator ruling (verbatim scope): raise the cap; protect-restorable / ephemeral-browse-rows / recency-instead-of-count were considered and NOT chosen.** **Landed:** `MAX_REGISTRY_SESSIONS` **32 → 256** (`registry.ts:72`). **Load-bearing finding — the constant was doing double duty:** `host.ts:640` used it as the HC4 **live-process** cap (`liveCount() >= MAX_REGISTRY_SESSIONS`), so raising the row bound would have silently raised the fork-bomb limit to 256 concurrent engine processes (~223 MB each per CC-5's measurement). Split into a new `MAX_LIVE_SESSIONS = 32` in `app/shared/hostApi.ts` beside the existing HC4 rate cap — **effective live limit unchanged**; security baseline preserved, not weakened. Same silent-widening avoided in `devHarness.ts:215`, whose debug-snapshot parse cap was `MAX_REGISTRY_SESSIONS * 4` and would have gone 128 → 1024; pinned to a literal 128. **Measurement (the ruling asked for evidence, not a guess)** — real module driven at N rows, rows shaped from the operator's live registry (~300 B/row), every timed write asserted to have reached disk: 32 rows = 10 KB / `launch()` 0.53 ms / write 0.39 ms; **256 = 98 KB / 1.20 ms / 0.50 ms**; 1024 = 397 KB / 2.87 ms / 0.81 ms; 4096 = 1.6 MB / 10.2 ms / 2.21 ms. So the old A5 "accretion hygiene" justification does not survive contact — A5 is handled independently by the immediate reap of rows that never ran a turn — and **no perf argument distinguishes any value below ~1024**; the measurement fixes only the affordability ceiling, the value itself is a product choice. **Invariants preserved:** live rows still never reaped; the `'parked'` IDLE-PARK exclusion untouched (`registry.ts:578`). **Failing-then-passing test:** `registry.test.ts` "opening a session with 32 restorable rows evicts nothing (a READ must not destroy state)" — at the old bound it fails on the behavioral assertion (`expect(reaped).toEqual([])` → `["app-r-0"]`, a restorable row destroyed), passes after; plus `host.test.ts` "the live-process cap is independent of the registry row bound" as a re-fusion tripwire. **Honest test fixes (not loosened):** `host.test.ts` F5 bound-reap hardcoded `32` and silently stopped exercising the reap once the bound moved → re-seeded off the constant; the F5 failure was caught by the battery, not assumed away. **Battery:** `bun test app/` **1343 pass / 0 fail** (measured baseline 1341/0 on the same tree — the worktree needed `bun install`; a stale-deps run showing 32 fails was environmental, not code) · `typecheck` clean · `typecheck:sidecar` 0 owned (5548 upstream ignored) · `test:hardening` **19/19** · `renderer:build` ok · `maps:lint` passed · `git diff --check` clean. **Docs corrected in place:** `decisions/REGISTRY.md` §3 (new value + measurement table + the withdrawn A5/latency rationale), §6.1 + HC4, A5; `decisions/SECURITY-MINIMUM.md` HC4 (now names `MAX_LIVE_SESSIONS`); `decisions/IDLE-PARK.md` line-anchor re-sync forced by my +15-line edit (`:85/:491/:570-609/:578/:777-786/:961-969`, `host.ts:636,664-666`). **⬜ Known residual, accepted + documented:** a *count* cap cannot separate browsing from keeping, so at 256 opened-and-closed sessions eviction resumes; eliminating the class needs one of the three shapes the operator rejected. **⬜ No GUI acceptance run** (headless only; the observable is that browsing many history rows no longer drops restorable ones from the sidebar). |
| **CC-7 · Engine-injected turns render as the operator's own messages: surface `MessageOrigin` over the wire** → `src/utils/messages/mappers.ts`, `src/QueryEngine.ts`, `src/entrypoints/sdk/coreSchemas.ts`, `app/shared/protocol.ts`, `app/renderer/src/transcriptProjector.ts`, `TranscriptView.tsx` | ✅ **LANDED 2026-07-26** (merged `ba23218`, pushed) · ⬜ operator GUI acceptance PENDING | **Bug (operator-visible):** messages the user never wrote render as their own pink right-aligned bubbles. `MessageOrigin` (`src/types/message.ts:10-41`) has **six** kinds; five are engine-injected but carry `role:'user'`. The desktop handled only `task-notification` (2026-07-22 bug-sweep #4) and `<command-message>` echoes (`transcriptProjector.ts:1593-1595`) — **`coordinator`, `channel`, `teammate`, `deferred-continuation` all fell through to `{kind:'user-text', role:'user'}`** and rendered `flex justify-end` (`TranscriptView.tsx:1132-1138`). **Root cause:** `origin` never crossed the wire (absent from BOTH `engine-types.snapshot.d.ts` and `sdk-types.snapshot.d.ts`), so the projector could only sniff message TEXT — `isTaskNotificationBanner` (`transcriptProjector.ts:1629`) was a hand-copied mirror of the engine's `isTaskNotificationText`, with a doc-comment admitting the copy (repo mistake #10). **Operator ruling: send the field over the wire; per-kind text matchers were considered and REJECTED** (four more engine-logic copies that break silently on wording changes). **Landed:** additive optional `SDKUserMessage.origin?: SDKMessageOrigin` — the frame already carried the message whole, so **no `PROTOCOL_VERSION` bump**; absent still means "the operator typed this", correct for every pre-field emitter (precedent: `tool_use_result`, `mappers.ts:139-145`). Outbound-only: no inbound frame kind, no preload channel; `SDKUserMessageSchema`/`StdinMessageSchema` have no runtime `.parse()` consumers (grep-verified) so widening them adds zero inbound surface — the change *removes* the renderer's need to infer provenance from attacker-influenceable text. **Narrowed at the engine, not forwarded whole:** `channel.meta` is DROPPED — its keys come from a third-party MCP server and `scanForSecrets` matches on key *names*, so a key named `authorization` would fail the guard and cost the entire frame (the same keys-as-values trap that makes `SettingsSnapshot.resolved` an array); `teammate` keeps only the handle, `task-notification` only `status`/`summary`. Two `secretGuard.test.ts` tests assert both halves (what ships passes; the un-narrowed version would not). **Live-path correction — the brief's assumption was wrong:** mid-turn-drained origin messages get `isMeta:true`→`isSynthetic` and the projector already drops them; the REAL leak is `QueryEngine.ts:961-978` yielding `queued_command` attachments as `SDKUserMessageReplay` with no `isSynthetic` and no `origin`. Both that and history replay (`toSDKMessages`) fixed. **Real-store probe before deleting the text mirror** (160 transcripts / 372 injected turns): **0 structured banners without `origin`, 372 with, all 372 non-meta i.e. visible** — that count is the bug's actual blast radius, and deleting the structured-banner sniff regresses nothing measured. Only the frozen `<task-notification>` XML check kept for legacy transcripts. **Latent bug closed en route:** `src/utils/messages.ts` called `taskNotificationOriginFromText` with **no import and no local definition** — a `ReferenceError` on the fallback path (one of two root-tsc errors that disappeared). **Three exhaustiveness tripwires proven by deliberate removal + restore** (projector switch → `TS2322 … not assignable to 'never'`; fixture mapped type → `TS2741`; new engine tripwire in `toSDKMessageOrigin`). **Failing-then-passing:** simulating the pre-fix wire fails **9** projector tests with the exact bug signature (`kind:"user-text"` where `injected-turn` expected). **§0 flags:** `coordinator` + `deferred-continuation` have **no TUI precedent** (isMeta-hidden / rendered as human turns — the latter IS this bug), headings taken from the engine's own `wrapCommandText` → `🔁 adapted`; teammate handle rides neutral accent because Tailwind cannot take an interpolated per-teammate color (the `AGENT_DOT_CLASS` dynamic-class trap) → `🔁 adapted`. **⬜ UNVERIFIED LIVE:** only `task-notification` exists in the operator's store; `coordinator`/`channel`/`teammate`/`deferred-continuation` need an orchestrator/swarm or channel session — headless per-kind coverage only. `2026-07-21-phase4.5-bug-sweep.md` §Bug 4 got a dated SUPERSEDED note (its wire claim is now false) rather than a rewrite. |
| **CC-8 · Terminal `/rename` never reaches the desktop (permanently, once a row is opened)** → `app/renderer/src/sessionsCatalogState.ts`, `app/host/registry.ts`, `app/shared/protocol.ts`, `app/shared/hostApi.ts`, `app/sidecar/sessionsCatalogDomain.ts` | ✅ **LANDED 2026-07-26** (merged `507be2f`, pushed) · ⬜ operator GUI acceptance **DECLINED 2026-07-26** (operator: *"i wont check it causme im too lazy. skip to next check"*) — the terminal-`/rename`-round-trip check was offered and passed over; not verified, not waived-as-correct | **Bug:** renaming a session from the terminal never shows in the desktop. **Two writers, neither sees the other:** terminal `/rename` writes the engine transcript ONLY (`src/commands/rename/rename.ts:57` → `saveCustomTitle`), never the host registry; `pickTitle` (`sessionsCatalogState.ts:250-254`) returned `registryTitle` whenever non-empty; and the `session-title` frame is *deliberately* not part of attach/replay (`sidecarServer.ts:2484`), so nothing ever re-read the transcript name. **Narrowing that survived the fix — a pure-history row was never broken** (no registry title → the cascade's `customTitle` wins), so only rows **opened in the app** were stuck; the trigger that gives a row a registry title is `openHistorySession.ts:117` seeding it from the catalog on open-from-history — i.e. the far side of bug-sweep Bug 2, whose tab-label fix is what now blocks renames. **Precedence rule chosen: newest *recorded* intent wins** — transcript title wins iff `entry.transcriptTitle` non-empty AND `catalog.capturedAtMs > (descriptor.titleUpdatedAt ?? 0)`, else registry title, else the catalog display cascade. **No existing timestamp could serve** (checked first, not assumed): the `custom-title` JSONL entry carries none (`sessionStorage.ts:2964-2976`), and `modifiedAtMs` is not a proxy because `enrichLog` overrides file mtime with the last in-file `timestamp` (`sessionStorage.ts:5550-5554`) and a title append has none — renaming an idle session does not move it. So two additive signals, both minimal: `SessionsCatalogSnapshot.capturedAtMs`, stamped at the **start** of enumeration so "captured after the registry stamp" *provably* implies the read saw the newer title (stamping at the end would let a pre-rename run win); and `RegistrySession`/`SessionDescriptor.titleUpdatedAt`, additive control-plane field following the `lastMessageSentAt` precedent (`hostApi.ts:87-95`). **Two decisions carry the correctness:** (1) `transcriptTitle` is a NEW field, not the existing `title` — `resolveEntryTitle` is a cascade (`customTitle > summary > firstPrompt > basename`), so letting *that* outrank the registry would replace a real app title with prompt text the moment the `ai-title` entry scrolled out of the bounded head/tail windows; (2) `titleUpdatedAt` stamps **only on genuine change** — restore replays a row's own title through `upsertOnSpawn` (`host.ts:332`), and re-stamping there would let an untouched title outrank a newer rename on every reopen. Desktop renames still win because they write both sides and stamp. **Failing-then-passing:** disabling the new `pickTitle` branch fails 3 tests; 4 companions (desktop rename not reverted, cascade cannot outrank, unknown-age snapshot never outranks, history row still works) pass in BOTH states — they pin what must not change. **Scope note (asked and answered):** 32 files / +694 = **9 load-bearing** (+231/−15), **6 test** (+437), and **17 files of pure fixture churn** (+26 total, one-to-five lines of `titleUpdatedAt: null`) — the tail exists because both new fields are **required** on the shared types, matching house precedent (`lastMessageSentAt`, `cwdExists` are both required-with-explicit-default) rather than optional; deliberate, and reversible for a smaller footprint. **⬜ Residuals:** surfacing is bounded by the catalog timer (~30 s), not instant — a push path was judged out of scope for a display fix; the registry title stays **stale on disk** (display corrected at read time, consistent with never mutating stored rows — a durable reconciliation would be a cross-process write from a stale read, the DR-2 trap). Pre-existing `protocol.ts:1929` doc drift corrected in passing. |
| **CC-9 · Desktop trust prompt understated its scope (approving one folder trusts every sibling in the repo)** → `app/sidecar/workspaceTrustDomain.ts`, `app/shared/protocol.ts`, `app/renderer/src/StartupSurfaces.tsx` | ✅ **LANDED 2026-07-26** (merged `fae69c5`, pushed) · ⬜ operator GUI acceptance PENDING | **Not a migration-introduced bug — a presentation mismatch that IS ours.** Trust is written at `getProjectPathForConfig()` = `findCanonicalGitRoot(getOriginalCwd())` (`src/utils/config.ts:1626-1638`), i.e. **the git root, never the session cwd**; the read checks that key first then walks up (`config.ts:752-778`, `isPathTrusted` `:790-799`). So approving in `repo/packages/foo` writes one key at `repo` and every sibling package reads trusted — in the desktop AND the terminal CLI, which share the global config. Git-root-keyed trust is upstream Claude Code behavior; it bites here only because **D4 ruled the desktop gate is per-session-create**, which *presents* as per-folder while storage is per-repo. **Operator ruling: fix the wording, leave the engine alone** — changing where trust is stored, or keying the app at the exact cwd (two trust models over one shared config file), were considered and REJECTED. **Landed:** new `executor.getTrustRoot()` returning `getProjectPathForConfig()` — deliberately **the same function that keys the write** (`saveCurrentProjectConfig` reads it at `config.ts:1675`), so displayed scope cannot drift from stored scope and the git root is never re-derived in `app/`; read in its own `try/catch` independent of `trusted` (matching how `detectedRepo` is handled); carried as additive `WorkspaceTrustSnapshot.trustRoot` on the **existing** `workspace-trust.snapshot` outbound frame — no new frame, no version bump, no inbound vocabulary. Three cases all covered: root wider than cwd (names the root inline + sibling/CLI consequence), root == cwd, and root **unresolved** (states the uncertainty rather than asserting a narrow scope it cannot prove). **⚠ Process note — the first round shipped an unrequested visual and was reverted.** It restructured the single path box into a two-row labelled scope block (`SESSION FOLDER` / divider / `TRUST IS SAVED FOR`) and self-flagged it `🔁 adapted` in the after-report — the same flag-it-and-ship-it pattern that produced the 2026-07-20 sidebar status chips the operator reversed on sight. Reverted to **wording-only** on instruction: the path box is byte-identical to pre-change, net visual delta is **one `<p>`** styled like the gate's existing intro. A tripwire test now enforces it (`trust gate keeps ONE Workspace path box — scope is carried in the copy, not extra rows`), asserting exactly one `<code>` element and the absence of the added labels across all three cases. Root cause of the deviation was an under-specified dispatch prompt (mine), not agent judgment. **P4-25 fail-closed re-verified:** `sidecarServer.ts` byte-identical (`git diff --stat` empty), `-t "P4-25"` passes, plus two new tests locking in that a throwing `getTrustRoot()` yields `trustRoot:null` with `trusted:false` and never discards a known `trusted:true`. **⬜ Residuals:** Settings → Workspace (`WorkspaceTrustSection.tsx`) now has `trustRoot` available but does not render it — left alone deliberately (the ruling scoped this to the consent moment); and the pre-existing engine asymmetry stands, unfixed and out of scope — the domain READS via `isPathTrusted(cwd)` (walks up from session cwd) but WRITES via `getProjectPathForConfig()` (memoized off `getOriginalCwd()`); they coincide only because the sidecar is spawned in the session cwd (P3-1), so a future divergence would write trust where the read never looks. Displaying the true write key makes such a divergence visible rather than silent. |
| **CC-10 · Terminal-created sessions take ~51 s to appear in the sidebar (A2, unparked)** → `app/main/sessionsCatalogRunner.ts` | ✅ **LANDED 2026-07-26** (merged `f277249`, pushed) · ⬜ operator GUI acceptance PENDING | **Supersedes the CC-5 "A2 PARKED" note.** Operator measured ~51 s against a 30 s target in the 2026-07-22 GUI run. **Both standing theories REFUTED by measurement before any code changed** — this bug already had two refuted hypotheses on record, so a blind fix was explicitly off the table. (1) *"the live run is slower than the hermetic harness"* — **dead**: the real worker runs **0.45 s** end-to-end against the real corpus (60 project dirs / 337 enumerable transcripts / 1.0 GB), split as engine-graph import 326 ms + `initializeSidecarRuntime` 46 ms + `enumerateSessionsCatalog` 288 ms; RAM-0's ~0.9 s was pessimistic. (2) *"the delivery hop is slow"* — **dead**: `onCatalog` → `sendHostEvent` → `contents.send` → `dispatchSessionsCatalog` is synchronous with no batching or throttle (`main.ts:394,714`; `App.tsx:673`), the read path has no cache (fresh `readdir`+`stat` per run), and the transcript writer flushes at 100 ms. (3) A hermetic end-to-end probe measured the app's **entire** contribution at **2.14 / 5.96 / 11.00 s** against a 30.36 s ceiling. **Where the ~20 s actually is — upstream, and structurally invisible to the app:** a transcript file is only created on the **first user/assistant message** (`materializeSessionFile`, `sessionStorage.ts:1290,1319`) and a `hasConversation === false` row is dropped (`sessionsCatalogDomain.ts:181`). Corroborated across 120 real transcripts: `(first user/assistant timestamp − file birthtime)` has **p50 −0.13 s** — the file comes into existence *at* the first message. A stopwatch started at "I created a session in the terminal" therefore counts operator typing + first-turn time before anything exists to enumerate, which also explains the reported *range* (~30–51 s) rather than a fixed number. **Honest boundary:** items 1–3 are measured; the residual attribution is proven negatively (provably not in the app's refresh path) plus positively via the materialization gate, but the operator's stopwatch start cannot be reconstructed. One in-app alternative also brackets 51 s — a **single failed run** cost a full extra interval (~60.7 s pre-fix) and failures reach only stderr; no log survives from 2026-07-22 to distinguish them. **Fix — neither prior recommendation was right as stated** (CC-5's "lengthen the interval" makes it worse; "fixed cadence" is correct but buys 0.36 s, not 20 s). What fixed cadence actually buys is closing a **latent unbounded-period bug**: the next run now anchors to the current run's **start** (`Math.max(0, intervalMs - (now() - startedAt))`) instead of its completion, so the period is exactly `intervalMs` instead of `intervalMs + runDuration`. Completion-anchoring let the period grow to `SESSIONS_CATALOG_WORKER_TIMEOUT_MS` — a **5.5-minute** refresh for a hung run — silently violating the ratified CATALOG-OWNERSHIP §4 "one interval + one boot" contract. Single-flight untouched (next run still only *scheduled* after the current settles; an overrun clamps to delay 0 rather than compounding); the one-shot main-supervised shape is unchanged, no locked decision reopened. **Re-measured after:** observed period between run starts **30.000 / 30.001 / 30.000 / 30.000 s** (was 30.36). **The 30 s interval value was deliberately NOT changed** — operator-owned tuning knob with a RAM tradeoff, mean latency already ~15 s. **Failing-then-passing:** 2 colocated tests fail pre-fix (`30000` vs expected `29550` / `0`); one also asserts `maxConcurrent === 1` so the fix cannot regress single-flight. **⬜ Residuals with numbers:** app-side worst case still ~30.0 s / mean ~15 s, bounded by the interval (cost per run 0.45 s wall / ~340 ms CPU / ~307 MB transient, nothing resident between ticks). **⬜ Open, worth a decision:** a failed run still costs a full extra interval (~60 s) with no retry or backoff, and failures are only visible on stderr — if A2 recurs live that is the first thing to check, and there is currently no way to check it after the fact. |
| **CC-11 · Codex diagnostic appenders mint orphan transcripts (they were LIVE, not latent)** → `src/utils/sessionStorage.ts`, `src/utils/cleanupRegistry.ts` | ✅ **LANDED 2026-07-26** (merged `d378d25`, pushed) · headless-only, no GUI observable | **⚠ Premise correction — this row exists because the lead got it wrong twice.** The task was dispatched as "close a latent landmine: these recorders have zero call sites." **False.** `recordCodexSendPath` and `recordCodexStreamSurface` (`sessionStorage.ts:465,502`) are invoked from `src/services/api/codex-fetch-adapter.ts:255,1668` and were actively firing. **Why the lead's grep lied — a repo-wide hazard worth knowing:** `codex-fetch-adapter.ts` line ~80 contains a **literal NUL byte** in a string constant (`STICKY_WILDCARD_ACCOUNT`, a deliberate sentinel written as a raw byte rather than an escape), so `file(1)` reports the file as `data` and **`rg` and `git grep` skip it silently** — all 3,634 lines invisible with no warning. Swept 2026-07-26: it is the **only** such file in `src/`. The failure is also *invocation-dependent* (a directory-walk `rg` can surface hits while piping the same file through stdin prints only `binary file matches`), so an apparent hit does not prove completeness either — use `rg -a` for anything load-bearing in that file. Fixing the sentinel to `'\\0any'` is behaviourally identical and would restore searchability; **not done** — another session had uncommitted edits in that exact file. **Severity was higher on this branch than on `main`:** `main` guards the send-path callback with `if (!isPrewarm && onSendPathComplete)`, `migration` has only `if (onSendPathComplete)` — and prewarm sends fire **before any user message**, precisely the orphan-minting window. This is the mechanism behind the 162-of-514 conversation-less transcripts the operator trashed on 2026-07-20. **Landed:** ownership predicate `getOwnedTranscriptPath()` gating on `Project.sessionFile !== null` — **not invented**, it is the same signal the engine already uses for this exact bug class (`sessionStorage.ts:879-881` "prevents metadata-only session files"; `insertMessageChain` `:1349` materializes only on a real user/assistant message; `adoptResumedSessionFile` `:1878` reasons about orphans in its own doc-comment). Three properties fall out free: it writes to `sessionFile` itself rather than re-deriving, so it can never target an unmaterialized path mid-`switchSession`; it inherits `shouldSkipPersistence()` (test env, `--no-session-persistence`); and it reads the module singleton directly so a diagnostic never constructs a `Project` as a side effect. **Extended to `recordPromptCacheBreak` (`:626`, also live via `promptCacheBreakDetection.ts:821`)** — both branches now closed against orphan *creation*; the agent branch uses `getOwnedAgentTranscriptPath()`, grounded in the existing `activeSubagents` registry (`cleanupRegistry.ts:41`, entries carry `transcriptPath`; registered at `AgentTool.tsx:1186` / `resumeAgent.ts:306`). `cleanupRegistry.ts` is **+12/−0, purely additive** (one exported read accessor; `registerCleanup`/`runCleanupFunctions`/`register`/`unregisterActiveSubagent`/`flushStallDetectedEntries` untouched — **no cleanup or shutdown behavior changed**). **Regression caught in its own WIP:** the first agent-branch guard was over-tight — `LocalMainSessionTask.ts:102` links its own agent transcript and passes that `agentId` to the API at `:125` but never calls `registerActiveSubagent`, so a backgrounded task's cache-break diagnostics would have been **silently dropped**; fixed with an existence fallback. Deliberately NOT closed by registering that task in `activeSubagents`, because `flushStallDetectedEntries` (`cleanupRegistry.ts:61`) iterates that map on exit and would emit spurious `stall-detected` records — a real behavior change needing its own ruling. **Failing-then-passing:** reverting the guards fails 2 tests ("no owning session writes nothing", "an unregistered agent writes nothing"); three positive-coverage tests pass in BOTH states, proving the guard costs no legitimate diagnostic coverage. **Battery:** focused suites 149/0 across 7 files · `build:dev:full` green · typecheck delta measured by restore-then-reapply, **zero new** (1895 total / 48 in touched files, identical both states). **⬜ Residual, stated precisely:** on the agent branch the second tier is an *existence proxy*, not an ownership assertion — an unregistered agentId whose derived path already exists will be appended to. It cannot **create** a file, so no orphan is minted, but it could append to a stale transcript under a recycled id. Closing that fully needs every agent-shaped caller in one registry, which is the `LocalMainSessionTask` ruling above. |
| **CC-12 · The sidebar offered sessions that cannot be opened (`resume-failed` on click)** → `app/host/host.ts`, `app/shared/limits.ts` | ✅ **LANDED 2026-07-26** (`c7b32a6`, local `migration`, not pushed) · ✅ **operator GUI-verified 2026-07-26** — created a session, typed nothing, closed it: its sidebar row vanished and no in-use session was collaterally dropped (operator: *"yeah it working."*) | **Bug (operator-reported, reproduced from the live registry):** clicking or restarting certain sidebar rows failed with `[sidecar] resume-failed: … no conversation found (transcript missing or unreadable)`. **Mechanism, re-verified in source:** the engine materializes a transcript only on the FIRST user/assistant message (`src/utils/sessionStorage.ts:1354` `materializeSessionFile`, called from `:1387`), but the registry stamps `engineSessionId` from the READY frame i.e. at spawn (`host.ts` `onSupervisorEvent` → `registry.fillEngineSessionId`). So every session opened and never typed in holds a non-null `engineSessionId` pointing at a file that does not exist. The launch reap already drops those (`registry.ts` `transcriptMissing`), but only BETWEEN launches — and `isRestorable` explicitly relied on that ("the launch reap already dropped rows whose transcript is gone"), so a row that acquired its id DURING the run was never re-checked and stayed advertised as restorable for the rest of the run. `restoreSession` did guard (`registry.hasTranscript`, §9-A4); **`restartSession` did not** — and it is the path that bumps `restartCount`, matching the operator's `restartCount: 2` and two identical failures. **Live blast radius: 1 of 26 rows carrying an `engineSessionId` had no transcript anywhere on disk** (measured read-only against `~/.cat-code/desktop/registry.json`); small but self-inflicted — every never-typed session becomes one. **Landed (3 changes + 2 required consequences):** (1) `isRestorable` now consults `registry.hasTranscript` at READ time, so the descriptor finally matches its own declared contract (`hostApi.ts` "Registry row + transcript both present", REGISTRY.md §6.1) — this also covers a transcript pruned mid-run; (2) `restartSession` gained the same guard, closing the asymmetry with `restoreSession`; (3) the exit handler classifies `RESUME_FAILED_EXIT_CODE` and retires the id for the run (in-memory `resumeFailed` set, no registry-schema change) — this is the only signal for the class `existsSync` cannot see: a transcript FILE that exists but carries no loadable conversation, exactly what the sessions catalog already drops as `hasConversation === false` (`sessionsCatalogDomain.ts` — the doctrine matched, not invented). Consequences the tests forced: `listSessions` and `closeSession`/spawn-failure now filter on the descriptor's OWN `restorable` flag rather than a second copy of the predicate — without this a just-closed no-transcript session came BACK as a live-looking tab, because the renderer folds `restorable === false` into TAB membership (`shellState.ts` foldTabMembership). The CRASH path deliberately still emits a status and keeps its tab (restart-in-place is the point). `RESUME_FAILED_EXIT_CODE` moved from `app/sidecar/index.ts` to `app/shared/limits.ts` beside `PARKED_EXIT_CODE`, per that constant's own "defined ONCE so exiter and classifier never drift" doc. **Perf judgement (asked for, measured not guessed):** `hasTranscript` is one `existsSync` per NON-LIVE row, and only `listSessions` fans out. Measured on the real machine: **256 rows = 0.138 ms warm / 0.111 ms all-missing per `listSessions()`**. Hottest caller is `idleParkDriver.evaluate` (each host event + a 60 s timer) → negligible. The one real risk was `canPreview`, which called `listSessions()` and is itself called once per cached file by main's startup `gcTranscriptCache` — O(rows²) ≈ 35 ms of main-thread `existsSync` at 256/256. Mitigated by deriving `canPreview` from THIS id's descriptor (provably equivalent: the union keys on the same flag), not by adding a cache. **Tests (red-before-green, 3 bounded mutations, each restored):** `host.test.ts` — never-typed session is not offered (mutating `isRestorable` → *Expected false, Received true* on the union assertion); `restartSession` refuses a no-transcript resume (mutating the guard → *Expected false, Received true*); `RESUME_FAILED_EXIT_CODE` retires an id whose FILE exists (mutating the classifier → *Expected false, Received true*), with an ordinary crash as the contrast control. Renderer projection proven through the real `reduceShellState`, not asserted on internals. One existing fixture corrected honestly (`F4 restartCount` never wrote a transcript → now does, like its sibling restart test). **Battery:** `bun test app/` **1430 pass / 0 fail** · `typecheck` clean · `typecheck:sidecar` 0 owned (5546 upstream ignored) · `test:hardening` **19/19** · `renderer:build` ok · `maps:lint` passed · `git diff --check` clean. **Security:** no new inbound frame, no preload channel, no protocol change — baseline trivially intact. **Stale refs swept:** `IDLE-PARK.md` ×3 (constant location + the now-conditional `isRestorable` claim), `spawnConfig.probe.test.ts` comment. **⬜ No GUI acceptance run** — operator steps: open a session, do NOT type in it, close it → the row must NOT appear as a restore-offer in the sidebar (pre-fix it appeared and failed on click). **⬜ Known residual (pre-existing, not a regression):** a session parked by IDLE-PARK before it ever ran a turn has no transcript, so unpark-on-click is refused — `restoreSession` always refused it; the descriptor is merely honest about it now. |

Both fixes are pinned only by exact-expression source tripwires. **These two need an operator keypress or a DOM harness (harness needs sign-off).** **Other HIGHs closed:** an unusable deferred-continuation store blocked *every prompt in every session* and the advertised recovery threw the same error; a crashed `submitted` job bricked the session permanently (16 of 60 real project dirs on this machine hit the triggering 0755 check); autocompaction made a *successful* continuation report "Stopped, needs you"; CLI `--resume` never armed the provider-switch lock, so a GPT session could be moved onto Anthropic mid-conversation; the session inspector was mounted without `sessionState`, so workspace trust / live permission rules / effective settings / flags / diagnostics rendered **nowhere** after CC-19 deleted them from Settings citing that inspector; one oversized frame **erased the whole replay ring**; the catalog and accounts-pool workers were never killed on quit, orphaning a ~189 MB engine-graph process invisible to every reaper; `remoteSettings.directConnect` accepted any renderer string as a URL (renderer-originated egress + session cwd, defeating `connect-src 'self'`; **predates the window**); writing an overridden setting turned that row into a permanently uneditable `unknown`; ⌘K with the Tasks dialog open killed a running background task. **Test-quality finding worth its own note:** the regression test for the 30-of-32 lost-run-facts outage drove a file the fix never touched and passed on the pre-fix tree; the line actually fixed had zero coverage. **Swept clean (do not re-audit):** em dash across `app/renderer/src` (910 hits, all comments), the Tailwind v4 dynamic-class trap (131 interpolations, 0 traps), feature/migration wiring (4/4), `src/codex-core/` (untouched all window — rotation machinery intact). **Deletions parked, not made:** `WorkspaceTrustSection` / `DiagnosticsSection` / `BannerStack` / `ResolutionOrderLegend` all have zero importers, but `STATUS.md:43`, `backlog/phase4.md:828`, `INVENTORY.md:90` and the 2026-07-21 cut-list audit treat them as live work — and `settingsScope.ts:710-716` says the replacement inspector was still awaiting its wiring, so the data rendered in **neither** place. Deleting would have destroyed the only implementation. **Battery:** `bun test app/` **1769 pass / 0 fail** (baseline 1711) · `app typecheck` clean · `typecheck:sidecar` clean (5545 upstream ignored) · `test:hardening` **19/19** · `build:dev:full` green · root typecheck 1896 (baseline 1897). **⬜ Open:** ~25 findings unfixed, incl. autocompact misclassification, the `mock.module` poisoning (an `afterAll` restore does NOT work — bun installs module mocks at import time; needs DI or a `.probe.` rename), `mainSource.test.ts` executing zero production code, the text guard scanning only `app/renderer/src` despite `2e71168` claiming repo-wide, and `sessionController.ts:206-232` carrying the same settings-beats-env defect in the desktop plane. **WAVE 3 (same session) closed the ~20 residuals** in 7 more commits `10dfbef`..`9b7ef70`. Battery after: `bun test app/` **1799 pass / 0 fail** (148 files, from 1711/142 at the sweep's start) · app typecheck clean · sidecar clean · hardening 19/19 · `build:dev:full` green · root typecheck 1896. **Two results worth reading even if nothing else here is:** (1) the old `app/main/mainSource.test.ts` was proven worthless by mutation — with the renderer bridge changed to never translate an event, single-use cwd tokens made infinitely reusable, and backfill reading the oldest rows instead of the newest, **all 18 of its tests still passed**; the replacement Electron-free module (`app/main/mainDecisions.ts`) fails 4 tests on those same three mutations. (2) the deferred-continuation suites reported 31 pass / **16 fail** when run together while each file was green alone, because bun installs `mock.module` at IMPORT time for the whole invocation — **an `afterAll` restore cannot work** (measured, twice). Fixed by injecting the durable writers through `QueryEngineConfig`; those five files are now **56/0** together. Three further leaking suites were found and deliberately NOT fixed (`Settings/Usage.test.tsx`, `Settings/Settings.test.tsx`, `codex-core/accounts.test.ts` — 10-13 unconditional module mocks each, and the last is the §6-fenced cross-process token file). **The `feature()` divergence is now quantified and is the biggest open item:** 911 call sites / 87 flags in `src/` all evaluate FALSE in the sidecar because it is spawned unbundled; `./cli-dev` enables 37 of those names covering 463 call sites, **436 of them outside the TUI**. Gate: topology chosen w/ packaged evidence (P0-1 ✅) · scaffold builds with tokens (P0-2 ✅) ·
types importable (P0-3 ✅) · **multi-session isolation known (P0-4 ✅)** · **security minimum drafted (P0-5 ✅)**.
→ **✅ GATE CLEARED WITH CAVEATS 2026-07-02** (whole-phase cold review: `reviews/2026-07-02-phase0-review.md`).
The two load-bearing decisions (Electron+Bun sidecar/IPC; N-process) survived adversarial attack and
are NOT reopened. But the review found handoff defects now **fixed in the backlog**: F1 (P1-0's
security line pointed at a stale ID — SECURITY-MINIMUM was orphaned) and F2 (code-home specified 3
contradictory ways). Carry-forward into P1-0/Phase-3 tracked in the backlog + review doc.
**Phase 1 (P1-0) is open.**

JSON stayed VALID — the atomic temp+rename write prevents tearing but NOT lost updates: engine-side `persistPermissionUpdates` writes back the full in-memory view without re-read/merge or a cross-process lock. At that point, the engine-side concern was not fixed in P3-5a and was **FLAGGED as a P3-8 rider.** Clearing P3-5a then required the engine-side read-merge/lock fix plus a same-cwd two-writer re-run confirming both rules landed with no loss.
**ENGINE-SIDE FIX LANDED 2026-07-05 (headless-proven, then GUI re-verified).** Root cause was two-part, not one: (1) `updateSettingsForSource` (`src/utils/settings/settings.ts`) had no cross-process lock around its read-merge-write — fixed with a `proper-lockfile` `lockSync` + bounded retry loop (`acquireSettingsLockSync`; `lockSync` itself cannot retry — "Cannot use retries with the sync api" — so the retry loop is required, not cosmetic). (2) The lock alone was proven insufficient (~1/3 of probe runs still lost a rule): callers (`addPermissionRulesToSettings`, `deletePermissionRuleFromSettings`, and the `addDirectories`/`removeRules`/`removeDirectories` cases in `persistPermissionUpdate`) pre-computed the full final array from a **cached, pre-lock** read, and `updateSettingsForSource`'s merge replaces arrays wholesale by design — so a stale pre-lock array still clobbered the other process's just-landed write despite fully serialized file access. Fix: `updateSettingsForSource` now accepts a `SettingsUpdater` function (`current => next | null`) invoked *under the lock* with the fresh on-disk read; all five read-modify-write call sites converted to this form (`setMode`/`replaceRules` correctly left as-is — no concurrent state to preserve). New two-real-process probe (`src/utils/settings/settingsWriteContention.probe.test.ts` + `.probe.child.ts`, house pattern per `accountRefreshContention.probe.test.ts`) calls the real production `persistPermissionUpdate` from two spawned processes racing one `settings.local.json`: **pre-fix ~2/3 runs lost a rule, lock-only ~1/3 still lost a rule, full fix 15/15 clean** (independently re-verified). `tsc --noEmit` zero new errors (diffed against clean HEAD); `bun test src/utils/settings src/utils/permissions` + `bun test app/` (368 pass) show no regressions (4 pre-existing unrelated failures in `enableAutoModeFlag.test.ts`, confirmed identical on clean HEAD). **GUI RE-VERIFICATION PASSED 2026-07-05 (human-driven, two sessions both in `/Users/pt/catcode-gui-scratch`, following the same-cwd two-writer protocol above).** Two fresh, distinguishable Bash commands (`race=A`, `race=B`) sent from two separate tabs at the same cwd, both reaching "Permission required" simultaneously; both approved via the "Always allow" suggestion (not the plain one-time "Allow" button — a driving mistake in an earlier automated attempt this same day caused a false non-result by only exercising one-time allow, never persisting a rule at all). Result read directly off disk: `cat /Users/pt/catcode-gui-scratch/.cat-code/settings.local.json` → both `Bash(curl -s "https://example.com/?race=A")` and `Bash(curl -s "https://example.com/?race=B")` present under `permissions.allow` — no loss. This closes the anti-Potemkin bar: headless probe (15/15) + live GUI same-cwd two-writer run both clean. **P3-5 SPLIT 2026-07-05 (5a of 2; prompt in `backlog/phase3.md`).** Root routing = real projection of the HostEvent stream + P3-4 stores (replaces AppV2 mock glue); establishes the shell's visual grammar 5b extends. Same-title "already open" is by-design (duplicate=focus). **ONE GAP FOUND → FIXED (operator-authorized, headless-proven; GUI re-verify of the relaunch check PENDING before ✅):** the saved split did NOT survive a full relaunch — on startup `reconcileWorkspaceLayout` ran against the not-yet-restored (restorable) sessions, dropped their panels, and the write effect re-persisted the degraded 1-panel layout, destroying the save before restore. **First (fragile) fix — REVERTED:** a `preservableSessionIds` set kept restorable panels in the active layout; GUI re-verify showed it made the panel COUNT survive but hid the live startup session behind "No sessions open" (preserved panels render nothing → `workspacePanels.length===0 → EmptyShell`, `App.tsx:731/794`), and clicking that session to escape reassigned a panel → wrong session restored. Order-dependent, not clean. **Proper fix (re-apply-on-restore, renderer-only):** the active layout degrades NORMALLY on relaunch (live startup session stays visible, no hidden EmptyShell); the disk-loaded MULTI-panel split is held in a `pendingRestore` state; the disk write is GATED while pending (so the startup degrade can't overwrite the save); `readyToRestoreLayout` (`workspaceLayout.ts`) snaps the saved split back ATOMICALLY once EVERY referenced session is live again — order-independent, overriding any interim clicks; pending is abandoned if a referenced session is unrecoverable (unblocks the write), and cleared on deliberate split/assign/close (so it can't clobber a new layout). Restore preserves the same `appSessionId` (proven in P3-7 check #3), so the referenced sessions match. New `readyToRestoreLayout` unit tests (both-live-snaps / partial-waits / empty-ignored). `bun test app/` **400/0**, renderer tsc clean. (Incidental: an earlier edit left a literal NUL byte in a `.join()` separator — made `grep` treat the file as binary and masked the char in reads; recovered by restoring `App.tsx` to the clean commit and re-applying. No NUL remains.) **✅ RELAUNCH GUI RE-VERIFY 2026-07-07 (cua-driver, AX-cited) — BOTH sequences PASS:** (A clean) split 2 panels (`cat-code c5aee5a5` / `app ec09a4ce`) → ⌘Q → relaunch → restore both → split re-formed identically, both `status:"ready"`; (B the previously-FAILING interaction) after relaunch the fresh startup session was VISIBLE in a panel (`Panel 1 … cat-code eaf298ad — active`, NOT "No sessions open"), then that fresh tab was CLICKED (the exact precondition that broke the fragile fix), then both prior sessions restored → split re-formed with the two ORIGINAL sessions, fresh session correctly stayed an unplaced 3rd tab (did NOT displace). Fragility no longer reproduces. |

Zero `resume-failed`. Anti-Potemkin (a)+(b)+(c) all hold per session per run. **BLOCKER FOUND & FIXED mid-gate** (this is the anti-Potemkin clause doing its job — a unit-test-invisible defect): first restore answered *"I don't have a magic word"* with `resume-seeded messages=1` (a lone `system` frame, no history). Root cause — the engine resume **tip-selection** picked the newest *non-sidechain* leaf, which is a trailing Codex `system` diagnostic (`codex_send_path` / `account.route.selected`, written `parentUuid:null` with a ms-later timestamp than the assistant turn); `buildConversationChain` from it collapses to 1 message, so a restored session held NO prior context. Fix (engine, surgical): `getLastSessionLog` (`src/utils/sessionStorage.ts:4275`) and its `.jsonl`-path twin `loadMessagesFromJsonlPath` (`src/utils/conversationRecovery.ts:427`) now select the newest **user/assistant** leaf, mirroring the already-correct `loadFullLog` (`:3358`). Missed by `resumeSeed.probe.test.ts` because its `recordTranscript` fixtures have no trailing dangling system frames. Regression test added (`sessionStorage.test.ts` → *"resume tip skips a trailing dangling system frame"*), tripwire-verified (reverting the fix → `firstPrompt:"No prompt"`). `bun test app/` **375 pass**, app tsc clean, sidecar tsc no new errors, hardening **19/19**. **TUI impact VERIFIED 2026-07-06 (headless, real transcripts):** the same bug hit TUI `--resume` of real Codex sessions — a real 1096-row session (`f5f183a0`) resumed with only **3 messages** of context pre-fix vs **203** post-fix (a second, `47a35d19`, 3→5). All TUI resume entry points (`main.tsx:3784` direct-UUID, `resume.tsx:235` `/resume`, plus `--continue`) funnel through the same `getLastSessionLog`/`loadFullLog`/`loadMessagesFromJsonlPath`, so **this commit fixes the daily-driver TUI too** — no separate change needed. |

2026-07-07: TUI parity + non-blocking reauth banner). Every session carries the §6 visual-fidelity
acceptance criterion; P4-4 is the P3 shell-fidelity true-up carry-forward.
