> covered the row, and never §0-flagged it**. By the ledger's own definition ⬜ requires a *named*
> owner, so a spent owner is no owner — those rows were ❓ wearing a ⬜ tag. **Operator ruling:
> own them, don't waive them** — *"i dont mind working it all. Just added it as a task in phase4
> then."* All 82 were re-tagged ❓ (marker: `promoted from ⬜ ORPHANED 2026-07-26`).
>
> **Read the numbers correctly.** Realized parity is **unchanged at 79%** — ⬜ and ❓ are both
> *in-scope* for the Realized% denominator, so the promotion is a pure ⬜→❓ transfer (268 ⬜ → 186,
> 45 ❓ → 127, in-scope **1,472 unchanged**). Part C grew because it became honest; **nothing was
> discovered, built, or lost in that step.** Do not read the Part C growth as a regression, and do
> not "fix" the number by demoting rows back to ⬜ — the ⬜ tag is exactly what let 82 rows hide
> behind spent owners.
>
> **Six new sessions own all 127: P4-29 … P4-34** (`backlog/phase4.md` TRANCHE G, rows below).
> When one lands, its rows flip **❓ → ✅/🔁 directly**. The Phase-4 gate still requires Part C
> **emptied or explicitly waived**; it is now *owned*, which is not the same as *closed*.
>
> **Their acceptance bar is not row-coverage.** Operator, verbatim: *"you are underestimate the
> goal of prototype. What we build should be rendered exact to prototype."* A session could flip
> all its rows and render nothing like the prototype — the 2026-07-07 Sidebar failure that created
> this ledger. Every TRANCHE G prompt therefore carries PROGRAM-PLAN §6 visual-fidelity acceptance
> (`PROGRAM-PLAN.md:323`/`:430`) in its DONE-WHEN, requires the `verifying-cat-code-changes`
> **FIDELITY** block + **SURFACE ACCEPTANCE** tiered verdict, and points at the render-and-diff
> runner `~/catcode_prototype/CatCode Web App.html`. An open, unapproved mismatch stops a
> fidelity-pass claim; a §0 flag is a *proposal* to the operator, never a closure.

Do not fix from this row until that is closed. **(2) A read action silently evicts restorable state.** Every open-from-history mints a registry row; live rows are never eligible for eviction, so at the cap the rows pushed out are genuinely-restorable recently-closed sessions. `2a6f39d` stopped *unopenable* transcripts from minting rows ("nine failed opens, nine reaps"); a legitimate open still takes a slot. The documented reason is A5 accretion hygiene (`decisions/REGISTRY.md:281`), which is already handled independently by immediate-reaping rows that never ran a turn; the file is ~4 KB at 32 rows, so no size/latency argument survives contact (an earlier "the sweep would get slower" claim in this session was an unmeasured inference and is withdrawn). The number was also sized when the registry was the ONLY session list — post-SESSIONS-UNIFICATION history is unlimited and the registry is just the open+restorable layer, so a *count* cap may be the wrong shape vs recency. Residual transient: the row may still briefly warp until the ready frame binds `engineSessionId` AND the next catalog run lands (≤30 s). Fully killing the transient = the listed "resolve the entry at mint" fix (now #4-owned, NOT CC-2/parked; GUI-observable → operator sign-off). On a die-with-window cold launch the active pane is a restored PREVIEW (no sidecar → no frame), and no persisted baseline existed anywhere → the page merged only the ~15 registry rows and looked complete. **Fix (4 parts):** **F1** — `selectSessionsCatalog` now returns active-slot → retained `latestGood` (freshest live snapshot from ANY session, which `lifecycle` no longer blanks — also fixes defect 5) → `baseline`; all App.tsx consumers flow through the single `sessionCatalogSnapshot` so SessionsPage rows + `catalogLoaded` + `welcomeRecents` + `activeSessionRow` all get the fallback. **F2** — cold-launch persisted baseline: the sidecar writes the global snapshot atomically to `<config-home>/desktop/sessions-catalog.json` after each successful enumeration (`app/sidecar/sessionsCatalogCache.ts`, path derived from the engine's own `getClaudeConfigHomeDir()` — the SAME source `registry.ts:173` re-implements; last-writer-wins, NO lock/merge — DR-2-trap-in-reverse avoided); main reads+validates it fail-closed (`app/main/sessionsCatalogBaseline.ts`) and exposes it via a new read-only HC3 preload method `readSessionsCatalog()` (fixed channel `catcode:host:sessions-catalog`, id-less, no renderer input); App folds it as a lowest-precedence baseline any live snapshot supersedes. NO new inbound frame kind, NO renderer-authored writes. **F3** — `🔁 adapted` (no prototype counterpart): SessionsPage shows a one-line inline notice above the list when `!catalogLoaded` even with registry rows present, so the list never looks complete when terminal history hasn't loaded. **F4** — NOT done (by design): terminal history in the SIDEBAR (registry-only). **⬜ OPEN PRODUCT QUESTION for operator:** should engine terminal-session history appear in the Sidebar too (not just the Sessions page)? Implemented nothing. **Live proof (headless, real `~/.cat-code`):** real sidecar enumeration→cache-write→main read-back round-tripped **513 entries** identically (vs ~15 registry rows today), and the sidecar write path == main read path == `/Users/pt/.cat-code/desktop/sessions-catalog.json` (both planes agree). The renderer baseline-fold is proven by unit tests (active-missing→latest-good; lifecycle keeps latest-good; baseline used when no live; baseline never clobbers live). The FINAL visual confirmation (Sessions page rendering ~513 rows with zero live sessions in the real Electron app) is operator-gated — a normal dev launch auto-creates a primary live session, so reproducing the exact zero-live cold-launch window needs the operator/harness. **Battery:** `bun test app/` **1232/0** · `typecheck` clean · `typecheck:sidecar` 0-owned (5547 upstream) · hardening **19/19** (bridge allowlist deliberately extended for the new read-only method — `hardening-smoke.ts` + `preloadSource.test.ts` 24→25 guards / 7→8 invoke channels) · `renderer:build` clean. **Files:** `app/renderer/src/sessionsCatalogState.ts`+test, `App.tsx` (selector fallback consumers + baseline mount effect), `SessionsPage.tsx`+test (F3 notice); `app/sidecar/{sessionsCatalogCache.ts+test,sessionsCatalogDomain.ts,sessionController.ts}`; `app/main/{sessionsCatalogBaseline.ts+test,main.ts}`; `app/shared/{sessionsCatalogCache.ts,protocol.ts}`; `app/preload/{preload.ts,preloadSource.test.ts}`; `app/scripts/hardening-smoke.ts`. |

| **P4-35** `starting` renders as a red failure in `ConnectionRecovery` (audit 24a) | ANY | 2 | ✅ **DONE 2026-07-31** | **Contract:** `starting` is the renderer's projection of the ONE send-failure code the wire marks `retryable: true` — `session_not_ready`, minted by `sendFailureCodeForStatus` only while the child is still `spawning`/`connecting` (`app/supervisor/supervisor.ts:509`; `SidecarSendError.retryable` `:127`; `ErrorFrame.retryable` `app/shared/protocol.ts:564`) — so it is transient by construction and must not paint a failure bar. **Derivation:** (1) producer is an **error** frame, not a lifecycle frame (`connectionState.ts:64-83`); (2) the existing classifier is `resolvePendingSubmit` (`composerState.ts:513-536`), which partitions the same union and `release`s exactly `dead`/`disconnected`/`failed`/`exited` — the new guard **aligns**, it does not deviate (`selectComposerGate`'s `connectPending` `:409-411` and `restoreConnecting` `App.tsx:3264-3266` group `connecting`+`starting` the same way); (3) `starting` is bounded — the spawn ends in `ready`, or in a terminal lifecycle frame via the F12 socket-wait cap (200 × 50 ms ≈ 10 s → kill + `failed`, `supervisor.ts:376-395`), `child.on('error')`→`failed` `:252`, `child.on('exit')`→`exited` `:264`; the ONE unbounded hole is a sidecar that connects its socket and then goes silent forever without exiting (no ready-frame handshake timeout after `setStatus(record,'connecting')`), and the composer's parked-prompt path already waits indefinitely in that same hole, so this changes no risk. **Fix:** new shared partition `isTerminalConnectionStatus` (`connectionState.ts:55`, own exhaustiveness tripwire) consumed by the guard (`App.tsx:4028`). Agreement with `resolvePendingSubmit` is pinned by test, so the two lists cannot drift. **Tests:** `App.test.tsx:1061` now covers all seven union members + the null-session case (proven red-then-green by reverting the guard); `connectionState.test.ts:158,165` add the partition + agreement tests with a compile-time completeness record. **Battery:** `bun test app/` 1996 pass / 0 fail (baseline 1994 + 2) · `bunx tsc --noEmit -p app/tsconfig.json` clean · `typecheck:sidecar` passed (5542 upstream ignored, zero owned) · `test:hardening` 19/19. **OUT as fenced:** the raw status word in the copy (26c class) and any connection tone grammar (24b, CC-5 ruling #6). No §0 deviations. Ledger §12 row "ConnectionChip: hidden when healthy" re-anchored. |

It is also the ONLY `role="alertdialog"` in production renderer source (swept), which is why the selector entry could not simply be dropped without weakening the general rule. **The fix is therefore two parts, and neither works alone:** (1) the target card takes focus on appear and hands it back on unmount if it still holds it (`PermissionPrompt.tsx:104-124`, the `PlanPanel.tsx:99-110` precedent); (2) that card carries `data-permission-key-host`, and the guard is now one exported predicate, `permissionKeysAreLive` (`permissionPromptModel.ts:24-78`), which treats the marked host as hosting the keys rather than owning them. **Listener ordering: unchanged, because the listener did not move.** It stays a `document` listener, and `dedicatedFlowOwnsKeyboard` (`App.tsx:2001-2003`) is untouched, so the double-resolve it prevents cannot come back — propagation is still target → document → window, and while `AskQuestionFlow` or `PlanPanel` hold their `window` listeners this one is not attached at all. The new focus target is gated on the SAME flag, so no generic card steals focus or advertises keys underneath a question or a plan. **P4-42's new disclosure button:** `button` is in the selector, so while focus sits on `Show input` / `Hide input` the keys are genuinely dead — and the hint now disappears in exactly that state rather than lying. Same for the Deny/Allow/always-allow buttons and, per the prompt's constraint, the deny-feedback `<input>`, where typing the letter `n` must never deny. Clicking back onto the card body returns focus to nothing, where the keys were always live. **Hint honesty:** the strip renders only while `keyboardTarget && keysLive && !denyOnly` (`:126`, `:253-257`) — one card at a time, never a card the shortcuts skip, never a state where a control owns the key. Renderer-only: no frame kind, no preload method, no protocol change, no new dep, security baseline untouched. ### GUI acceptance sitting — 2026-07-26/27 (operator-scoped)

**Operator scoping ruling (2026-07-26):** *"what about we only check what added today/this
session? and mark other as finished"*. Agreed scope = **11 checks** — the seven landed that day
(CC-5, CC-7, CC-8, CC-9, CC-10, CC-12, P4-34 Lane 2) plus four older load-bearing ones
(P4-18 Edit/DiffView/Grep/Stop/Esc, P4-5 accounts, P4-19 managed-policy, P4-24 composer).

**Everything outside those 11 is hereby WAIVED, dated 2026-07-26, attributed to the operator.**
Waived ≠ verified: the waive records that the operator chose not to spend a sitting on them, and
those rows must not be read as GUI-confirmed. Any future regression report against a waived
surface is a first observation, not a re-opened failure.

Two fixes landed mid-sitting and are the highest-value outstanding checks, because both are
pure-interaction changes and the `app/` renderer suite is SSR-only — no headless test in this
repo can press a key, fire focus, or run an effect, so their whole interaction layer is unproven:

- **CC-13** Settings → Permissions (fix `59ca42e`) — open Settings → Permissions with **no session
  open**. The pane must render rules immediately instead of *"Waiting for the engine's permission
  context…"*, and the "Default mode" heading must show the saved `permissions.defaultMode`, not a
  session's live mode.
- **CC-16** composer during connect (fix `2f4278d`) — open a not-live session and **type
  immediately**: the first keystroke must land, with no `Focus to reconnect…` / `Connecting…` and
  no dropped characters. Press Enter *inside* the connect window: the turn must start once ready.
  Then mid-turn, the composer must still refuse input and the send arrow stay grey.
- **CC-18** workspace reorder (fix `78e920b`) — the drag gesture, real `localStorage`, and restart
  persistence are all unreachable from an SSR suite. With ≥3 workspace groups in the rail:
  (1) hover the rail open, press-and-hold a workspace HEADING (the label, not a session row) and
  drag it over another heading — a 2px accent rule must appear on the edge it will land on
  (below when dragging down, above when dragging up) and the dragged heading must dim; release and
  it must land exactly there, with its sessions intact. (2) Confirm nothing else moved: the other
  headings keep their relative order and no row inside any group changed position. (3) Type in the
  rail's search box until only two groups remain, reorder those two, clear the search — the
  filtered-out groups must reappear in their previous relative order, not reshuffled. (4) Click a
  session in the LAST group: its group must NOT jump to the top (CC-2 warp-free). (5) Quit and
  relaunch Cat Code — the custom order must be exactly as left. (6) Open the Sessions page — its
  workspace grouping must still be plain alphabetical, unaffected by the rail's order. (7) Keyboard
  path: Tab/click to focus a workspace heading and press **⌥↑ / ⌥↓** — it must move one slot per
  press and stop at the ends. (8) The **Unknown workspace** group (only present if some transcript
  has no recorded workspace) must stay pinned at the BOTTOM and refuse to drag. (9) Drag a TAB to a
  pane edge — the split drop must still work, unaffected by the new drag type.

## How to update this file (for the finishing session)
1. Flip the session's **Status** (⬜→🟡→✅) and add today's date + a one-line note.
2. If you just cleared a **phase gate**, say so in that phase's header.
3. If you generated the next phase's backlog, add its sessions as rows here **with 🧠 tags**.
